<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Spirit of Mercury and Brother Albertus Bayr. Although the previous page provided was numbered 184, this page is marked 187, suggesting a brief gap in the manuscript or a numbering error. The conversation shifts from technical alchemical instruction to a stern warning regarding the dangers of the Work and the necessity of secrecy.</meta>

### With Brother Albertus Bayr. 187

...afterward reveal everything that you do not now understand. Be still and silent; show your Abbot your books no longer; tell him not a single word of our meeting, under penalty of life and limb. <note>original: *bey Leib vnd Blut*; literally "by body and blood," a common Early Modern oath emphasizing that breaking the secret would result in physical death or mortal sin.</note> Cast aside all sorcery <note>original: *zauberey*; many alchemists were careful to distinguish their "natural philosophy" from forbidden black magic or necromancy.</note> and do not summon <note>original: *beschwere*; in this context, it refers to the ritual act of "constraining" or "burdening" a spirit to appear.</note> me any longer. Remain in good intent; pray to GOD for grace and a good spirit, otherwise I may not come to you again. If you do this, I will be your good friend, and as often as you need my counsel, I will always be with you.

->Albertus.<-

Ah, stay just a little longer! Tell me: will I live to see us complete the **tincture**? <term>Tincture: A liquid form of the Philosopher’s Stone, believed to be capable of transmuting base metals into gold or granting longevity.</term>

->Mercurius.<-

Yes, you will complete it, but your Abbot will not live that long. You will obtain it after his death; and if you do not look out for yourself wisely, it will be the cause of your own death. Therefore, take good care of yourself. Watch closely to whom you show it, for this tincture will cause great delusion. Furthermore, you should guard your little books more diligently at all times than even the tincture itself; take care that they are never found on your person, for you could fall into great danger, imprisonment, and murder because of them. Therefore, be cautious and fare thee well.

->Albertus.<-

I, Brother Albertus Bayr, of the **Carmelite Order**, <note>A mendicant Catholic religious order. Alchemical practitioners were frequently found within monasteries, despite official church prohibitions.</note> affirm and testify herewith, before
[right-aligned] GOD

<summary>The Spirit of Mercury warns Brother Albertus to keep their meetings secret from his Abbot and to abandon sorcery, prophesying that the alchemical tincture will eventually be completed but will bring about Albertus's death if he is not cautious.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit of Mercury, Brother Albertus Bayr, tincture, secrecy, Abbot, prophecy, Carmelite Order, danger of alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the narrative of Brother Albertus Bayr, though the page numbering (188) follows the previous dialogue (187). It transitions from the direct transcript of the dialogue to a retrospective account of how the encounter with the Spirit of Mercury first occurred in 1568.</meta>

188 [space] ## Dialogue of the Spirit of Mercury
To God and his Angels, and dear Saints,
that in the year 1568, on the 18th day of February—which was the feast of the illustrious and highly blessed, ever-remaining Virgin Mary’s Candlemas <note>original: *Mariæ Liechtmeß*; Candlemas commemorates the presentation of Jesus at the Temple and was a significant date in the liturgical and alchemical calendar.</note>—as I was in my cell in the monastery, *Maria Magdalena of the New Star* <note>original: *Maria Magdalena de stella nova*</note>, such a vision appeared to me and held the aforementioned dialogue with me. This happened after I rose and went to bed day and night with **philosophical books** and thoughts, and begged God the Lord with fervent sighs day and night that He would graciously reveal the truth of this **Art** <term>Art: Often used to refer to Alchemy, specifically the "Great Work" of transmutation.</term> to me. There, in my ignorance—God forgive me—because I believed no differently, after I had worked 23 years in vain with great effort alongside my Abbot, and diligently tended the **fire** day and night, [I thought] that one could learn the secret of these things from no man, but must force it from the spirits; even though it is much more possible for men than for spirits, as I, praise God, found in the end.

Then, on the aforementioned day, through customary ceremonies and proper conjurations <note>original: *beschwerungen*; ritual constraints used to summon and bind a spirit.</note>—as is very common in monasteries in Italy and Spain—acting as a monastery Exorcist (God forgive me), I summoned the **spirit of Mercury** <note>original: *spiritum Mercurij*</note> and demanded a conversation. He appeared to me in the form of a black, elongated, hideous glimmer or shadow, without any form or shape of a man or animal, and to me with
[space] resound- <note>The text ends mid-word on "hallen-", likely intending *hallender Stimme* (resounding voice).</note>

<summary>Brother Albertus Bayr recounts how, after 23 years of failed alchemical experiments with his Abbot, he resorted to his skills as an exorcist to summon the Spirit of Mercury in 1568.</summary>
<keywords>Brother Albertus Bayr, Spirit of Mercury, Candlemas, exorcism, monastery, Maria Magdalena de stella nova, conjuration, alchemical failure</keywords>

--- Page 215 ---
<meta>This page continues the autobiographical account of Brother Albertus Bayr. Having summoned the Spirit of Mercury on the previous page, Bayr now describes the physical appearance of the spirit and the subsequent successful alchemical operation that resulted in the production of the "Matter" in 1571.</meta>

With Brother Albertus Bayr. • 189

...a resounding, ringing voice, giving question and answer as previously reported. And as I sat down at the table by his command, readying myself with pen and ink, that same shadow or black radiance stepped into the middle of the circle, regardless of the consecrated sword, the censed candles, and other ritual trappings <note>original: *gauckelwerck*; literally "jugglery" or "trickery." Bayr uses this term to dismiss the external ceremonies of ritual magic in favor of the internal alchemical truth he later discovered.</note>. Afterwards, it transformed from the black color, through ash-colored gray clouds, into a completely bright white radiance; and finally, it changed from white, through a light yellow color, into the highest redness.

The form and size, however, did not transform or change, but remained completely unmoved within the circle until the end of the conversation. In the middle of the radiance, the symbol of Mercury <note>original: *zeichen Mercurii*; the astrological and alchemical symbol ☿.</note> stood in three distinct colors.

Finally, when it disappeared as recounted, my cell appeared blood-red, both inside and out, much like the sun is accustomed to shine blood-red into a room. After this revelation, I arranged everything with my Abbot. Within two years, we produced eleven pounds and seven *loth* <note>A *loth* is a historical Germanic unit of weight, roughly equal to 15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of the true **Matter** with great effort and diligence, and happily completed the **Work** <term>Work: The "Great Work" (*Magnum Opus*), the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</term> in the year 1571, as I have since faithfully and clearly recorded. 

But my Abbot did not live to see it; for on the 2nd of June prior, he was found dead in bed beside his concubine. From beginning to end, I saw all the colors, and just as the **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> showed itself in the circle, so too did the three primary colors appear, namely: black

<summary>Brother Albertus Bayr describes the Spirit of Mercury’s transformation through the alchemical colors (black, white, yellow, red) and records the successful creation of the alchemical Matter in 1571, shortly before his Abbot's scandalous death.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit of Mercury, alchemical colors, Nigredo, Albedo, Rubedo, 1571, Abbot's death, magical circle, ritual magic, transmutation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Brother Albertus Bayr’s account of his alchemical work. After the initial appearance of the Spirit of Mercury, Bayr describes the guidance he received during the chemical transformations and his subsequent flight from the monastery to Augsburg and Nuremberg.</meta>

### 130 Dialogue of the Spirit of Mercury. With Brother Albertus Bayr.

...black, white, and red <note>These colors (nigredo, albedo, and rubedo) represent the primary stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note>, thus found distinctly in the work; and whenever a single error occurred, I always received good advice and instruction from the aforementioned spirit <note>original: *spiritu*</note>. In particular, however, he explained the parable of the Fountain of Bernard <note>A reference to Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist whose "Parable of the Fountain" describes the dissolution of the "King" (gold/soul) into a mercurial bath.</note> to me in such a way that afterwards all the hidden writings and figures of the Chaldeans and Egyptians and other philosophers became manifest to me—as I have recorded this for the most part with my own blood in the fourth part of my little book, which I wrote about the book of Bernard.

But after the completion of the work, I was unable to bring the spirit <note>original: *Spiritum*</note> back to me for several years. Therefore, the multiplication <note>original: *vermehrung*; the process of increasing the power and quantity of the alchemical medicine.</note> in strength and quantity proved very difficult for me. And because I received no further instruction or report from the spirit, and the other brothers, and especially the new Abbot, were very hostile and opposed to me—because they could learn nothing from me in these matters—I secretly stole away within a few years with my tincture and several old and good Egyptian books. I arrived safely in Augsburg, and thereafter traveled to Nuremberg, and was glad that I finally reached German soil, with the comforting hope that I would eventually find someone who would show me the augmentation <term>Augmentation: The alchemical process of increasing the potency or volume of a substance, often through further digestion or addition of material.</term>.

May God the Almighty further help everyone with His grace; highly praised and lauded for all eternity, Amen, Amen, Amen.

<note>At the bottom right of the page, a red circular library stamp is visible. It features a crown in the center, the letter "I" below it, and the words "BIBL. ROYALE" (Royal Library) around the inner border.</note>

<summary>Brother Albertus Bayr describes receiving alchemical secrets from the Spirit of Mercury and his eventual flight from a hostile monastery to Augsburg and Nuremberg, carrying his "tincture" and seeking the secret of augmentation.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit of Mercury, Bernard of Trevisan, fountain parable, augmentation, tincture, Augsburg, Nuremberg, alchemical colors, monastic life</keywords>
